Texts and Jami results 7
  1. «و اصطفاه على جميع العباد بتبليغ رسالته... و كان كعلمه فيه رءوفا رحيما», Amir al-Mu'minin at Siffin (Bihar 32:616-617; Nahj reads «لتبليغ... فكان كعلمه فيه»): Jami FOUND, wording as printed; already printed in full in B4 S16.
  2. «قال أنا عدل لا أجور» and «ولا ألزم أحدا إلا ما عرفته منه قبل أن أخلقه», the clay report (Bihar 5:231-232; Kanz al-Daqa'iq 9:439-440; Nur al-Thaqalayn 2:446): Jami FOUND.
  3. «الشقي من علم الله وهو في بطن أمه أنه سيعمل أعمال الأشقياء», Imam al-Kazim (al-Tawhid of al-Saduq): Jami FOUND; Jami also holds the Imam's answer to «اعملوا فكل ميسر لما خلق له» («إن الله عز وجل خلق الجن والإنس ليعبدوه ولم يخلقهم ليعصوه...»), which the recording lacks.
  4. «...فلما حكم بذلك وهب لأهل محبته القوة على معرفته... ووهب لأهل المعصية القوة على معصيتهم لسبق علمه فيهم... فوافقوا ما سبق لهم في علمه», al-Sadiq via Abu Basir (al-Kafi 1:203-204), with 8:23: Jami FOUND.
  5. 2:7 and al-Rida «الختم هو الطبع على قلوب الكفار عقوبة على كفرهم كما قال عز وجل بل طبع الله عليها بكفرهم» ('Uyun; al-Ihtijaj 396-397): Jami FOUND; the built block is from a late compilation and starts mid-sentence: take the Ihtijaj or Safi wording.
  6. «...سئل أمير المؤمنين عن القضاء والقدر... وكل سابق في علم الله» (al-Ihtijaj; Bihar 5:95): Jami FOUND, wording as printed (restored).
  7. Dua al-Nudba «بعد أن شرطت عليهم الزهد في درجات هذه الدنيا... و علمت منهم الوفاء به» (Iqbal al-A'mal): Jami FOUND. Not verified as printed (Jami check): «انتخبه و انتجبه» (Fadak sermon reads «اختاره وانتجبه قبل أن أرسله»); «لسبق علمي» NOT-FOUND (nearest is text 4, «لسبق علمه فيهم»); «سبقت العالمين الى المعالي بحسن خلقتي» is a verse (POEM), no speaker; the wall verse «أترجو أمة قتلت حسينا...» is a POEM told as a narration; «في علمك أني فاعل» is a cut of the istighfar «لكل ذنب سبق في علمك أني فاعله»; «منتخب کا منتخب» and the Imam Askari paraphrase have no Arabic (NOT-FOUND).
Next lesson picks up

کیا اللہ ہر چیز پر قادر ہے، اور اس کی قدرت کہاں تک ہے؟ (Is God able over everything, and how far does His power reach?) (S11)

4. His power S11 to S14

S11 اللہ کی قدرت اس کی ذات سے ہے اور ہر ممکن چیز پر بے حد ہے؛ ذاتاً ناممکن کوئی چیز ہی نہیں کہ اس کی حد بنے، نہ کوئی اس سے بچ سکتا ہے، نہ اسے روک، گھٹا یا تھکا سکتا ہے؛ اس لیے اسی سے بڑا مانگو اور اسی کی پناہ لو

ledger · 38 new · 9 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Is God able over everything, and how far? Over every thing, without limit: nothing in the heavens or the earth frustrates Him, if He wills He takes you away and brings a new creation; when He wills a thing He says to it "Be"; what He has made is not the measure of what He can do, and every drop and every thought is bewildered in His power (MM U7.21-1 P006:1-7 with 14:19-20 and 35:44; AH P005:9 «قدرت خدا وسیع است، مطلق است»; MM P061:3-8 with 39:62 as the one ayah, AH P062:1-2; 36:82 once, JS P063:8; EZ P012:1-3 one line; EZ U7.21-6 P247:4-14, P248:5-7 in prose; MM U7.21-3 P186:1-4 one line; points H.2, H.9, H.79, H.21). [H.2, H.9, H.79 NEW | H.21 PART: recall B1 S07 "everything is bewildered in His power, read there for 'everything knows God'" then new: the closing line «إلا وهي في قدرتك متحيّرات» read as a teaching on power]
  2. From where is this power, and is it a tool beside Him? From His Essence, before there was any object of power; over all that His knowledge discloses; asked "did God create things by power?", Imam al-Rida علیہ السلام answered that power is not a thing other than Him; "His reliance is on His power" means on Himself (MM U7.21-1 P004:1-2, P001:5-6 at the simple layer, one paragraph; JS P003:4-15 one line, Saduq: power over what does not yet exist is ownership; JS U7.21-5 P226:2-7, P227:1-15 for al-Rida's answer; MM U7.21-1 P022:9-16 last clause only, with JS U7.21-6 P241:9-16, al-Rida named; Teacher Copy: MM P017:1-2 (ra'y, H.3), JS U7.21-6 P238:2-13, P239:3-16 (H.76); points H.1, H.57, H.77, H.3, H.76). [H.1, H.57, H.77, H.3 NEW | H.76 PART: recall B4 S12 "EZ: 'I breathed into him of My spirit' does not mean a part of God came out" then new: JS's reading as an honorific annexation, «هي من قدرته»]
  3. Is He incapable of anything, and what is it to deny His power? He is free of every incapacity: an incapable being would need another; He is unable neither to say nor to do; whoever denies His power is an unbeliever; He showed us His power in our own selves; and no measure of His power reaches Him, «they did not measure God His true measure» (MM U7.21-1 P018:1-2; MM P019:4 as the block (al-Sadiq), P019:1 one line; AH P020:1-3 one line; JS P021:3 as a block, al-Sadiq to Ibn Abi al-Awja named, P021:4-13 one line; EZ U7.21-3 P165:5-9 and JS P164:1-8 with the 6:149 part sent to S15; MM U7.21-1 P022:7-8 «لا يوصف بعجز ... فلا يوصف بقدرة إلا كان أعظم من ذلك» with 6:91 (EZ P024:1-4) and 39:67 (MM P075:2 block, AH P023:1-3 inline) as one ayah block; points H.4, H.46, H.5; RR1-007 recalled). new
  4. What does "able" mean? Dominion over doing and leaving, joined with choice: He can do and can leave, like the hand that moves and rests at will; He gives the kingdom and takes it away; He is an agent not under compulsion. So to say "He must do" affirms power in word and denies it in fact: no afterlife as revealed, no erasure (AH U7.21-1 P077:3-18 as the carrier, 3:26 with AH's use P026:11 and P077:1; EZ U7.21-2 P109:4-34, P110:14-34, one example of qudra and quwwa; JS U7.21-1 P060:5-14, P043:11-19 one line each («له أن يفعل وله أن لا يفعل»); JS U7.21-2 P111:3 «فاعل لا باضطرار» with its source named; MM P025:2 and P027:1 told in Urdu (the verdict and two consequences); JS P122:5-9 one line; Teacher Copy: H.6 (MM P025, AH P026:1-16), H.7 (MM P027:1-6, JS P035 to P036), H.52 (JS U7.21-5 P217 to P223), H.55 (JS P224 to P225), H.78 (EZ U7.21-6 P245 to P246); points H.53, H.6, H.7, H.52, H.55, H.78). new
  5. What does power attach to? Every possible thing, every «شيء»; the essentially impossible is no thing at all, so it marks no limit on Him; the paradox questions (the world in an egg, a stone He cannot lift) are wrong questions, and the Imams' answers silence the questioner (MM U7.21-1 P017:1 and AH P062:1-2 as the thesis lines; EZ U7.21-2 P133:4-19; JS P126:1-14, P132:6-10, the mathematician once; JS U7.21-3 P166:3-24, P171:1-5, P173:7-18; al-Sadiq's pupil of the eye P176:2-24 cut to the point; EZ P172:6-20 one line; Teacher Copy: H.60 (JS U7.21-5 P229:1-6), K.27 (JS U7.24 P133:14 to P134:5), H.8 (MM U7.21-3 P180:1-5, Galen, with a reconciliation, ASK); points H.54, H.59, H.60, K.27, H.8). [H.54, H.60, K.27, H.8 NEW | H.59 PART: recall B3 S09, S10 "the Daysani's question and al-Sadiq's answer by the pupil of the eye; the egg as proof of a designer" then new: the paradox questions analysed under power, the answer judged a silencing one]
  6. Is the strange or unusual impossible, and do His means, His laws or His promise bind Him? No: the customary impossible is not the rational one; His power needs no prior material and no means; His hands are outspread; He is not overcome by His own laws; a promise does not bind His power, «إلا ما شاء الله»; we neither deny such a report nor believe it only because it is possible (EZ U7.21-2 P141:3-20, P142:2-8, P142:19-30; EZ P137:2-18, JS P136:16-26 one line; AH U7.21-1 P005:1-11 and JS U7.21-2 P148:5-16 for 5:64, with al-Rida's «لو كان هكذا لكان مخلوقا» P148:1-4 named; EZ P163:1-12, JS P162:1-8; JS U7.21-5 P210:2-7 with 87:7; EZ U7.21-3 P170:20-23 as the practical rule; signs (moon, staff, Maryam, Zakariyya) named in one sentence, told in S13; Teacher Copy: H.44 (JS P209:1-17, 11:107-108), K.20 (JS U7.24 P077), H.82 under its recall (EZ U7.21-6 P251 to P252); points H.58, H.56, H.75, H.39, H.44, K.20, H.82). [H.75, H.39, H.44, K.20 NEW | H.58 PART: recall B3 S12, S13 "the return of the sun is nothing impossible in so vast a creation; Ibrahim's fire; deniers measure resurrection by their own power" then new: the general rule that the customary impossible is not the rational one | H.56 PART: recall B3 S16, B4 S12 "He was not from a thing nor made what was from a thing" then new: His power is bound to no prior material, EZ's stalled industry | H.82 SAME: recall only, B5 S05]
  7. Can anything escape, resist, lessen or tire Him? None outruns Him, none He seizes slips away; everything is His army; who can own anything against God; disobedience lessens His kingdom in nothing and obedience adds nothing; hating His decree does not change it, and the one who turns away stays in need of Him; magic and oppression work by His permission, not His pleasure; respite is not incapacity; keeping the heavens and the earth does not tire Him, and He who can act whenever He wills has no haste; nothing resisted its own coming to be and nothing resists its passing away (Nahj 109 as one block: JS U7.21-1 P013:2-3, U7.21-2 P099:9, P104:11, P106:6-10, U7.21-5 P213:7; JS U7.21-2 P097:2-4 as JS's words, EZ P102:2-19 one line; JS U7.21-4 P205:2-8 with Nahj 90 named; JS U7.21-5 P212:1-17 with 5:17; JS P104:3-28; JS P213:2-9; JS P208:1-15 one line, detail to S15; JS P214:1-17 with 11:20, 3:178 and istidraj to S26; EZ U7.21-2 P161:5 with 2:255, JS P150:7-11; JS U7.21-4 P196:3-33 with 50:38; MM U7.21-1 P076:1 cut before the gnat and P076:3 «بغير امتناع منها كان فناؤها»; Teacher Copy: H.61 (JS U7.21-5 P230:19-22, P231:12-16, 40:16 one line); points H.37, H.40, H.47, H.48, H.49, H.43, H.50, H.33, H.34, H.12, H.61; RR1-015 and RR4-034 recalled, RR1-019 Teacher Copy). [H.37, H.40, H.47, H.49, H.43, H.33, H.12, H.61 NEW | H.48 PART: recall B1 S01 "creatures add nothing to God; if all disbelieved no dust would settle on His grandeur" then new: obedience and disobedience neither add to nor lessen His kingdom | H.50 PART: recall B3 S19 "the Quran states the custom of respite for oppressors" then new: respite is not incapacity, 11:20 | H.34 PART: recall B3 S16 "He could have created in the blink of an eye, but made it in six days so the angels infer Him" then new: haste comes from fear of losing the chance, and He has none]
  8. So what follows for us? Since nothing is too great for Him, ask much; when every road is shut He opens a door no mind reached; take refuge in His might that none can assail and His power that nothing resists (MM U7.21-1 P082:2-4, the core line «سلوا الله وأجزلوا فإنه لا يتعاظمه شيء»; AH P083:1-2, P084:1, kinder than a father; JS U7.21-2 P091:9, the refuge dua cut at «شيء»; JS U7.21-4 P203:4 one line; AH re-read RR5-020 (ZH-S12 ¶16) «پیش تو سهل است» one line; fuller dua teaching stays in S27; points H.15, H.38). [H.38 NEW | H.15 PART: recall B1 S18 "the most knowing of God ask Him most" then new: ask much because nothing is too great for His power, the two men in Paradise, AH's father]

Who carries it

MM carries steps 1, 2, 3, 7 and 8 (P006, P004, P018, P019:4, P022:7-8, P076, P082) and gives the thesis line of step 5 (P017:1); AH carries step 4 (the hand, P077:3-18) and joins steps 1, 3, 5, 6 and 8 (P005:9, P020, P062, P005:1-11 on 5:64, P083 to P084). Step 6 is EZ and JS apart from AH's 5:64 reading; the definitional additions of the second half (H.52, H.55, H.60, H.78) and most of step 7 are JS and EZ, where AH and MM give only the anchor lines (MM P076:3; AH and MM C1 lines named by the atlas but not gathered). JS gives the narrations AH and MM lack (al-Sadiq to Ibn Abi al-Awja, al-Rida on power as no instrument, Nahj 109).

Texts and Jami results 7
  1. «أولم يسيروا في الأرض ... وما كان الله ليعجزه من شيء في السماوات ولا في الأرض إنه كان عليما قديرا» (35:44; MM U7.21-1 P006:7), with 14:19-20 «إن يشأ يذهبكم ويأت بخلق جديد» (MM P006:3).
  2. «من شبه الله بخلقه فهو مشرك ومن أنكر قدرته فهو كافر» (Imam al-Sadiq; al-Tawhid; Jami FOUND, Mustadrak Safinat al-Bihar 5:351; the block prints MM's isnad frame inside the said text; MM P019:4).
  3. «ويلك وكيف احتجب عنك من أراك قدرته في نفسك نشوءك ولم تكن وكبرك بعد صغرك وقوتك بعد ضعفك ...» (Imam al-Sadiq to Ibn Abi al-Awja; al-Kafi; Jami FOUND, Mir'at al-'Uqul 1:247, Sharh al-Kafi 3:18; the recording has «وقمتك» for «وقوتك», and the Urdu was made from a Jami matn that starts mid-question; JS P021:3).
  4. «إن الله عز وجل لا يوصف بعجز ... فلا يوصف بقدرة إلا كان أعظم من ذلك» (Imam al-Sadiq and Imam al-Baqir; al-Tawhid, Bihar 4:142; Jami has both wordings: the printed excerpt «لا يوصف ... بقدره», Bihar 4:142 MM's «بعجز ... بقدرة»; MM P022:7-8), with 6:91 or 39:67.
  5. «إن الله تبارك وتعالى لا ينسب إلى العجز والذي سألتني لا يكون» (Amir al-Mu'minin on the world in an egg; al-Tawhid bab al-qudra h.9, Bihar 4:143; Jami FOUND-OTHER-WORDING: «لا يكون», not the lesson's «إنه لم يكن»; JS U7.21-2 P126:21, U7.21-3 P173:15, P176:25), with Imam al-Sadiq's answer to al-Daysani by the pupil of the eye (al-Kafi via Sharh al-Kafi 3:37), the Masum the lesson calls «آقا حسن ثانی».
  6. «سبحانك ما أعظم ما نرى من خلقك وما أصغر عظمه في جنب قدرتك ... ولا يسبقك من طلبت ولا يفلتك من أخذت ولا ينقص سلطانك من عصاك ولا يزيد في ملكك من أطاعك ولا يرد أمرك من سخط قضاءك ولا يستغني عنك من تولى عن أمرك» (Amir al-Mu'minin, Nahj sermon 109; Jami FOUND, Subhi al-Salih 1:92, Ibn Maytham 3:50, Bihar 4:318; P013 starts mid-clause, «ولا يستغني عنك» at P213:7 is the same sermon, not a separate dua), with Nahj 186 «هو المفني لها بعد وجودها ... بلا قدرة منها كان ابتداء خلقها وبغير امتناع منها كان فناؤها» (Jami FOUND; MM P076:1, :3).
  7. «سلوا الله وأجزلوا فإنه لا يتعاظمه شيء» (the Prophet صلی اللہ علیہ و آلہ, the two men in Paradise; Wasa'il from 'Uddat al-Da'i, Bihar 90:302; Jami FOUND; the printed excerpt stops at «لا يتعاظمه» before «شيء»; MM P082:2-4) and «أعوذ بوجهك الكريم وعزتك التي لا ترام وقدرتك التي لا يمتنع منها شيء» (al-Kafi 3:334, from «أبي الحسن» with 'Ali b. Mahziyar in the chain; Jami FOUND-OTHER-SPEAKER: not al-Kazim and not al-Sadiq as JS says; JS P091:9).

The steps 8
  1. Is rising after death possible at all? Reason finds no impossibility, and the first creation removes every ground for denial: "who will revive bones?" "He who originated them the first time". JS P003:1-10, P004:2-18 (one paragraph); 36:77-79 with Ubayy b. Khalaf and the crumbled bone (JS P006:2-8, P065:1-10). (H.64). ↺ B3 S13Already taught: B3 S13 "He who made from nothing revives; 36:80-81 and al-Sadiq to the zindiq"New here: 36:77-79 printed and the Ubayy b. Khalaf report (JS P006, P065)
  2. Before the return, does everything perish, and can what perished come back? All on the earth perishes and His Face remains; at the blast things perish, then He returns them as their Governor began them; as He began you, you return; the mind makes, unmakes and remakes an image with no strain. MM U7.21-1 P075:1-4 (55:26-27; al-Sadiq «بل هو باق إلى وقت ينفخ في الصور ... ثم أعيدت الأشياء كما بدأها مدبرها»); EZ/JS U7.21-2 P145:1-22 (39:68, 7:29), P146:2-12, P147:3-7 (the mind's library); restoring the non-existent in three lines (JS U7.21-6 P232:1-8, ASK). (H.11, H.63, H.62). [H.11 NEW | H.63 PART: recall B3 S13 "the Power that made the camel and the world will bring about the resurrection" then new: the return as He began by the same agent, and the mind that makes, unmakes and remakes an image (EZ/JS P145 to P147) | H.62 NEW]
  3. How hard is it for Him? Creating and raising all creation is like one soul; "easier" is our language; nothing is easier or harder for Him; the deniers measure by their own power. MM U7.22-1 P001:6-7 (31:28; the Prophet «لتموتن كما تنامون ولتبعثن كما تستيقظون», the "one soul" clause only after the attribution check); MM P002 (MM's verse list: 30:27, 31:28, 36:12); JS P045:4-9, P092:17-20 (two sentences); 50:15 (JS P035:20); the "far return" (50:3, JS P028:9-23, P030:13) as a recall line. (H.13). [H.13: core repeat kept in full, see Repeats; base recalled from B3 S13 (the same power that made man raises him; 50:3, the deniers measure by their limited power; 36:80-81), new taught: creating and raising as one soul (31:28), 30:27 as our language, 50:15]
  4. But the body is scattered: how is it gathered? Every particle stays in His knowledge and power; what the earth takes from them is known; the dust is kept in the earth with Him from whom not an atom is hidden. JS P019:8-22, 50:4 (P027:36), 75:3-4 (P006:15-16), P008:8-17; the zindiq hadith cut to its two clauses (JS P072:2-4); TC fine layer, one clause each: al-Sajjad (P071:2), Nahj 83 «أخرجهم من ضرائح القبور وأوكار الطيور» (P073:2), al-Sadiq on shrouds renewed (P074:2). (H.66, H.13 new part). [H.66 PART: recall B3 S13 "al-Sadiq's debate with the zindiq: the power that made the foetus returns him" then new: the scattered dust kept in His knowledge (the zindiq hadith's other clauses, al-Sajjad), raised from graves and nests (Nahj 83), shrouds renewed (JS P071 to P074)]
  5. What if one body becomes part of another? The original parts are kept; for one who knows creation by will alone, this doubt is dust. JS P050:1-3, P051:5-26 (the seed example); Khwaja Nasir's line in Urdu only; MM P049:2 as the landing. (H.14). new
  6. Which body rises? This earthly one, by the explicit text. JS P045:1-3, P090:1-5; JS P092:1-3 (Ibrahim); TC: the Mulla Sadra line (P090:31-35), ASK. (H.65). new
  7. Has He shown this already? The man made to die a hundred years and raised, his food unchanged: «أعلم أن الله على كل شيء قدير»; and Abraham's "show me how You give life to the dead", which JS places in the chapter of power. 2:259 cut (EZ P086:4), EZ P086:18-43 cut to the facts the ayah gives, JS P083:6; MM P049:1 (Tafsir al-Qummi, al-Sadiq; Jami names the man Irmiya, not Uzayr); JS U7.21-4 P195:3-13 (2:260). (H.68, H.32). [H.68 NEW | H.32 PART: recall B1 S14 "Abraham's 'that my heart be at rest' (2:260), there to rank Ali's certainty above Abraham's" then new: 2:260 read as a display of the power to revive (JS U7.21-4 P195:3-13)]
  8. Is there a daily sample of it? Sleep and waking, the green tree's fire, the dead earth revived, the living brought out of the dead: a display of His power, not a law of nature. JS P091:7-12 with «لتموتن كما تنامون» (back-reference to step 3), 36:80 (reprinted, flagged), 57:17 (reprinted, flagged); TC fine layer: 30:27, 46:33, 75:40 and the cited scholars (P092); EZ U7.22-2 P096:1-14 (30:19, display of power, against the Hegelian use; P096:15-35 aside ASK). (H.67, H.86). [H.67 PART: recall B3 S13 "the power that formed life from dead matter gathers it again; 36:80-81; spring reviving the dead earth" then new: «لتموتن كما تنامون» as the daily sample, 30:27, 46:33, 75:40 (JS P091, P092) | H.86 NEW]

Next lesson picks up

کیا اس کے لیے کوئی کام دوسرے سے زیادہ مشکل ہے؟ (Is anything harder for Him than another?) (S13) Note on the counts: SAME recalled = 0 points + 2 SAME RR records (RR1-004, RR1-005); PART 6 points, of which H.13 is the core repeat and 5 are base-recalled; NONE 9 points, of which 6 are taught (H.11, H.62, H.14, H.68, H.65, H.86) and 3 are held (H.88, H.89, H.90); Arabic reprinted 2 = 36:80-81 and 57:17.

S13 اللہ کے لیے کوئی کام دوسرے سے مشکل نہیں: اس کی نشانیاں دکھاتی ہیں کہ دور کو قریب کرنا، بھاری کو ہلکا کرنا اور عادت سے ہٹ کر کام کرنا اس کی قدرت کا کام ہے، اور یہی قدرت ہم پر بھی ہے

ledger · 15 new · 4 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. Is anything harder for Him than another? No: keeping the heavens and the earth does not tire Him, and before Him a galaxy is less than a lamp bulb is to us; His creation shows it: angels the earth could not hold, the Throne that all the angels could not move until He said «خلوه علي أمسكه بقدرتي» and «أنا الله المقرب للبعيد ... والمسهل للعسير»; the angel half fire and half snow praising «يا مؤلفا بين البرد والنار»; night made to enter day (EZ U7.22-2 P098:21-24 with the 2:255 clause «ولا يؤوده حفظهما», EZ P099:7 with 14:20, EZ U7.22-1 P047:1-26 one example only; MM U7.21-1 P085 and JS U7.21-4 P191 one narration; MM U7.21-2 P086:1-5 as the one Throne block, U7.21-3 P182:9-15 as its frame; MM U7.21-2 P086:6, :17; JS U7.21-4 P194:4-8 with 57:6 one line; AH U7.21-2 P087:8-19, diamond from coal, one line; H.16, H.28, H.22, H.17, H.18, H.31). new
  2. Is the fine as easy for Him as the vast? Yes: when Adam asked how his back held all his progeny, the answer was «بلطف صنيعي ونافذ قدرتي»; and had He willed to make Adam of a light that dazzles the eyes «لفعل» (MM U7.21-3 P183:2-13, the two sentences on Adam's question and the answer; MM P185:6-10, Nahj 192; H.19, H.20). [H.19 PART: recall B2 S14 "the Rawha narration: God drew Adam's progeny from his back like bees, for the covenant" then new: Adam's question «وكيف وسعتهم ظهري» and the answer read for power. H.20 NEW]
  3. Then is every unusual report possible? What is impossible by custom is not impossible by reason; nature is His habit, not His chain; He can, but does not always, because He chooses; so al-Baqir calls the returns (karrat) power, denied only by the Qadariyya (EZ U7.22-2 P102:12-24, JS U7.22-1 P087:6-11 one paragraph; EZ P102:6-11 with 11:118 as the bridge to S14; MM U7.21-3 P181:1-4 one line «تلك القدرة ولا ينكرها إلا القدرية»; H.10). [H.10 NEW; H.93 SAME: recall only]
  4. What has He shown? The sun returned for Ali's prayer; the night journey, «أسرى بعبده», He took His servant in body; AH's picture of the host who prepares the best in his power for a beloved guest, and God's feast for His beloved by essence; one pointer line to the given-power reports homed elsewhere (EZ U7.22-2 P097:14 to P099:9 cut, the Bilal hypothetical one line; JS U7.22-1 P054:20-28, P078:3-19, P077:1-12; EZ P079 the brick story as the one story; MM 7.14.8 one line; AH U7.21-3 P188:1-15; EZ P105 Eid garments one line in the TC; H.87, H.72, H.24, H.92; pointer to H.88, H.89, H.90, held in H12). [H.87 PART: recall B3 S12 "within so vast a creation the return of the sun is nothing impossible (JS)" then new: EZ's Bilal adhan with the solar system held still, and the sources he cites. H.72, H.24 NEW. H.92 PART: recall B1 S10 then new: the lizard and the Eid garments read as proofs of power]
  5. Is a long life too much for Him? He who kept Yunus alive in the fish keeps His proof; Imam al-Hasan: «يطيل الله عمره في غيبته ثم يظهره بقدرته في صورة شاب ... ليعلم أن الله على كل شيء قدير»; Salih went old and came back young, «يمحو الله ما يشاء ويثبت» (AH U7.22-1 P053:1-3 carrier; EZ P089:14 with 37:143-144; EZ P064:16-19 one block; JS P058:10-21 boxwood one line; AH U7.21-3 P189:9-10 and the lines after it with 13:39; H.25, H.26; H.71 TC only). new
  6. How do the Imams call on this power? Imam Musa al-Kazim in Harun's prison: «يا مخلص الشجر من بين رمل وطين وماء ويا مخلص اللبن من بين فرث ودم»: he asks the Able to free him as He frees the tree from the clay (EZ U7.22-2 P113:18-24, the cut restored; H.94). ↺ B3 S12Already taught: B3 S12 "tender plants break concrete and asphalt by His power (EZ)"New here: the Imam's prison dua, P113:18-24
  7. What does it mean for me when I hold power? «واذكر ... عند القدرة قدرة الله عليك»; the Prophet to Abu Mas'ud beating his slave: «اعلم أبا مسعود أن الله أقدر عليك منك عليه»; one Urdu line from al-Najashi's letter, He is its Guardian and Able over it (EZ U7.22-2 P114:5-13, the dictator-signature line only; EZ P111:1-11; EZ P115:14 as one Urdu line; H.95, H.92 (Abu Mas'ud), H.96). new

Next lesson picks up

کیا اللہ سورج کی طرح ہے جو چمکے بغیر نہیں رہ سکتا، یا وہ چاہے تو کرے اور چاہے تو نہ کرے؟ (S14)

S14 اللہ فاعلِ مختار ہے، مجبوری سے کرنے والا نہیں: کرنا اور نہ کرنا دونوں اس کے اختیار میں ہیں، اسے کوئی چیز مجبور نہیں کرتی، اور جو وہ دیتا ہے اس پر ہمارا کوئی حق نہیں

ledger · 16 new · 5 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Does He act as the sun shines and the flower gives scent, or can He also not act? Not like the sun: if creation issued from His Essence by itself He would be like the sun that must shine; His grace flows like a spring, yet by His choice (one-line recall of S13 step 3, "He can, but does not always"; EZ U7.24-1 P091:3-12 the flower; EZ P073:20-24 the sun, one analogy only, JS P098:13-28 as the alternative; back-reference to the U7.1 question «involuntary, like perfume?», U7.1-1 EZ P008:5-9; K.28; K.34 one line). new
  2. What is a free agent? One who knows, is pleased, is not compelled, and owns both doing and not doing; choice belongs to His Essence, and His will (mashiyya) is its exercise, the filter every gift passes through; Amir al-Mu'minin: «فاعل لا باضطرار» (JS U7.24-1 P030:3-6, P033:4-23 one paragraph; JS P078:4-7 as the key text; JS P046 and EZ P101 one line; AH's hand turned both ways recalled from S11 in one line, U7.21-1 P077:3-6; K.8, K.12, K.24). [K.8 PART: recall B3 S01 "the Maker is an Agent, not a Cause (AH)" then new: choice as dominion over doing and refraining, mukhtar against mujib. K.24 PART: recall B3 S14 "perfections are given from beyond nature by His mashiyya" then new: mashiyya as an attribute of act, the filter of every gift. K.12 NEW]
  3. Is doing and not doing a lack in Him, or a perfection? A perfection: of two beings, the one who can do and leave is the more perfect; the philosophers changed the question into "must He act?", while revelation puts His acts under His will; "cause" is said of Him only loosely (JS U7.24-1 P034:3-14, P064:1-3; JS P030:1-14 in one plain sentence; teacher note EZ P089, agent of causes not cause of causes, the brick and the boy; K.22, K.23). [K.22 NEW; K.23 PART: recall B3 S01 "AH: Agent, not Cause; causality and congeneity are false" then new: "cause" only lexically (JS), «عامل العلل» not «علة العلل» (EZ P089), teacher note only]
  4. Where do the Quran and the Imams say it? «إنما أمره إذا أراد شيئا أن يقول له كن فيكون»: the act hangs on «إذا أراد»; «إن يشأ يذهبكم ويأت بخلق جديد»: He did not will, so He did not; Imam al-Sadiq: «ثم إن الله ... أحب أن يخلق خلقا»: He was and had not acted, then acted (JS U7.24-1 P067:4-9 with 36:82; JS P068:6, :19-24 with 14:19; JS P095:19-22, cut before «فقال كونا ظلين»; K.19 verses, K.25). new
  5. But does not every choice need a reason that tips it? No: choice is itself what tips it, and an act chosen among a thousand reasons is still chosen; the options are nothing before His choosing (AH U7.24-1 P027:1-26, the mosque and the two cups; MM P038:1 in one plain sentence; EZ P035:1-19 the blind man as the one story; K.11). new
  6. Then does His wisdom, or anything else, compel Him? No: His will is caused by nothing and conditioned by nothing but Himself, yet it is never without wisdom; «لا يسأل عما يفعل»; wisdom and benefit follow His act and do not force it; nothing binds Him unless He binds Himself (AH U7.24-1 P048:1-5 with 21:23 once; AH P056:1-4; K.13, K.16). new
  7. Do we then have any claim on what He gives? No: we were «لم يكن شيئا مذكورا», and He is «يا مبتدئا بالنعم قبل استحقاقها»; why I was born here or there is His wise will; the prophets' first rank is His choice, like inherited capital; if He wills He gives prophetic knowledge at birth, and if not, five hundred years avail nothing (AH U7.24-1 P054:1-8 with 76:1 and the du'a named; EZ P055 the seed, one line; AH P063:1-22; AH U7.24-2 P122:1-19, P123:4-10 with one «من يشاء» ayah, 20:41 not made an address to the Prophet; AH re-read RR3-040, ZH-S29 ¶87-91; K.15, K.19, K.18; K.17 teacher note). [NEW; K.17 PART: recall B3 S15 "AH: differences of effects go back to His measuring; His choice is the specifier" then new, teacher note only: creatures' limits are accidental, by His permission and pleasure]
  8. What does this ask of us? He wants faith by choice: Imam al-Rida on 10:99, «لكني أريد منهم أن يؤمنوا مختارين غير مضطرين»; our own choice is His sign in us, He free by His Essence and we by Him; power is from Him and the decision ours, no jabr and no tafwid (JS U7.24-1 P096, P097:12-17 with 10:99 once; JS U7.24-2 P099:3-8, P100:3-8, P103:7-10; EZ P105:4-22 one paragraph with the parrot line and «لا جبر ولا تفويض» named; K.26, K.29, K.33). Landing for S15 (the two wills). new

Next lesson picks up

کیا گناہ گار کا گناہ بھی اللہ کی مشیت سے ہے، اور اگر ہے تو اسے ملامت کیوں کی جاتی ہے؟ (S15)

5. His will S15 to S16

S15 اللہ کی دو مشیتیں ہیں: تکوینی مشیت کبھی نہیں ٹلتی اور تشریعی مشیت کی نافرمانی ہو سکتی ہے؛ مگر نافرمان اللہ پر غالب نہیں آتا، کیونکہ اس کا اذن رکاوٹ نہ بننا ہے، اس کی رضا نہیں

ledger H15 · 11 new · 0 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Is the drinker's drinking and the tyrant's wrong by God's will, and if so how is he blamed? Start from a stone and a person: a stone that strikes you is not asked "what do you want of me?", a man who slaps you is; the whole question stands on the difference between a thing that is moved and an agent who chooses; it is the question that left whole schools helpless. (EZ H1 P062:1-11, P038:1-9, P103:25-39; B7.I.57 lead-in). new
  2. Does God have one will? Two: «إن لله إرادتين ومشيئتين: إرادة حتم وإرادة عزم», the creative will by which things come to be, and the legislative will by which He asks something of a chooser. (JS H1 P054:7-14, P057:1-8; EZ H1 P103:1-39; B7.I.57; the Fath narration printed once, P054 and P033 as one block per diag). Fine layer: a legislative revelation passes into creation once the Prophet speaks it, and command, request and asking by rank (EZ U7.37-4 P158:3-24; B7.J.59; P158:2 is a broken sentence to fix). new
  3. Then how can He command and not will, forbid and will? The same narration states it: «ينهى وهو يشاء ويأمر وهو لا يشاء، أو ما رأيت أنه نهى آدم ...»; the puzzle is posed here and answered in steps 6 to 8. (JS H1 P033:13-17, :20-21, P060:1-15; B7.I.57). new
  4. Which will can never fail? The creative will: «إنما أمره إذا أراد شيئا أن يقول له كن فيكون» (36:82); «والله أعز من أن يريد أمرا فلا يكون» (al-Baqir and al-Sadiq, P044 as a block); 61:8 as the creative will in history; at Siffin Amir al-Mu'minin praised Him who does not undo what He has fastened, and "had He willed, this umma would not have differed": by His creative will He could stop the difference, He did not decide to gather them so. The legislative will can be refused. AH's voice: Amir al-Mu'minin to Umm Kulthum on the night of the 19th, "one cannot stand against God's decree" (RR2-022), and the couplet «گر شود ذرات عالم حیله پیچ / با قضای آسمان هیچ است هیچ» (RR4-036, new part). Teacher Copy: the two wills apart and together, the four cases (JS munfarid); «قلة الامتناع» as a teacher note. (EZ H1 P044:1-15, P109:10, P056:24-34; JS H1 P043:4-14; EZ U7.21-6 P255:2-7, :13-16; JS P171:1-16, P172 for B7.I.59; JS P194, P195 for B7.I.68; B7.I.58, B7.H.84, B7.I.59, B7.I.68). [NEW; RR4-036 PART: recall B3 S11 "no escape from what heaven decrees, in the Pharaoh story" then new: the couplet, ZH-S28 ¶137-139; RR3-010, RR3-011 SAME: recall only]
  5. When I disobey, do I overpower Him? No: «لم يطع بإكراه ولم يعص بغلبة» (al-Rida); He could block and does not. One traffic image only: the city allows the road (mashiyya), wants the lights obeyed (irada), does not stop the car (idhn), and the fine comes at its time (ajal); the other five tellings go to the Teacher Copy. Teacher Copy, one line: an intense desire does not compel; the philosophers' chain of wills back to an eternal will is named only (MM 7.32.23, B7.M.53, M2+). (EZ H1 P004:12-49 with P004:42 the block; JS H1 P002:1 trimmed to «أمر تخييرا ونهى تحذيرا ... ولم يعص مغلوبا ولم يطع مكرها», P003:5-23 with the Nahj du'a «ولا ينقص سلطانك من عصاك»; MM H1 P001:2 only per Ask; EZ U7.30-2 P196:2-28 for B7.I.76; EZ H1 P021:1-23 for B7.I.66; B7.I.23, B7.I.76, B7.I.66). new
  6. Then what is His "permission"? Not blocking, and wider than His pleasure: obligatory acts have His command, permission and pleasure; sins His permission only; al-Rida: «أما الطاعات فإرادة الله ومشيته فيها الأمر بها والرضا لها والمعاونة عليها»; Amir al-Mu'minin's three states of acts (fara'id, fada'il, ma'asi). Teacher Copy: AH's Harun story, God did not stop the display of Musa b. Ja'far's body so the decree would run and the Waqifa be exposed: permission without pleasure (RR5-016). (JS H1 P013:8-29, P009:4-24, P117:2-6, P092:5-15 re-cut to the answer; EZ H1 P012:9-23, P017:1-28 as TC repeats of JS; B7.I.61). [NEW; RR5-030, RR4-011, RR2-028 SAME: recall only; within Bab 7, AH's B7.K.17 (H14, TC) recalled in one line]
  7. Why command what He will not let happen? Some commands are a test; the test shows the servant to himself and to others, it does not teach God. Teacher Copy: MM's two lines from other Fasls of this Bab, B7.N.2 (U7.39-2, commands from ra'y for testing) and B7.E.25 (U7.13, His test is not to learn), one line each. (JS H1 P063:4-27, P022:1-8, P023:1-3; EZ H1 P073:10-61; B7.I.101). new
  8. So what of Adam forbidden the tree and Ibrahim told to sacrifice? One reading for the Handout: JS ranks the creative will in both cases best (P096) and the testing command next (P066); the rest (P075, P069 with the «تصمیمی» ASR doubt, P080, P081, P173 to P179) to the Teacher Copy. (JS H1 P096:2-13, P066:6-33, P075:11-34, P069:43-50; EZ H1 P078:1-18; the B7.I.58 Adam and Ibrahim rows). [NEW; RR1-045 SAME: recall only]

Yazid): al-Kafi via Masabih al-Anwar 131; Jami FOUND (UNVERIFIED #265, #266); the ref list should lead with al-Kafi; Jami's matn names «إسحاق» (Ask).

Next lesson picks up

کیا جس چیز کا اللہ حکم دیتا ہے وہ ہمیشہ اسے محبوب بھی ہوتی ہے، اور «إن الله شاء أن يراك قتيلا» کا کیا معنی ہے؟ (Is everything God commands always loved by Him, and what does "God willed to see you slain" mean?) (S16)

S16 اللہ جس کام کا حکم دیتا ہے وہ ہمیشہ اسے محبوب نہیں، اور جو کچھ اس کے اذن سے ہو جاتا ہے وہ ہمیشہ اس کی رضا نہیں؛ کربلا، ہدایت و گمراہی، آیۂ تطہیر اور حکمرانوں کا اقتدار اسی فرق سے سمجھ میں آتے ہیں

ledger H16 · 16 new · 1 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. Is everything He commands loved by Him? No. After a one-line recap of S15 (what He wills for us against what He wills from us: prayer is willed from us, the heat for us), the answer: He may hate a servant yet love his deed, and love a servant yet hate his deed (al-Baqir to the Shami); prayer and war are both commanded, prayer is loved and lengthened, war is ended as soon as one can; the finger cut for the body's sake is a duty and not loved; martyrdom too is not loved for itself. (EZ H2 P198:11-19, P198:44 one line; EZ H2 P204:1-14; P205:4-39; P206:10-30 without the lisan al-hal verse P206:31-36, ASK; B7.I.77, B7.I.89). [B7.I.77 NEW (one line, bridge) | B7.I.89 NEW] The Abu Basir report (H1 P120, «شاء وأراد وقدر وقضى ... وأحب؟ قال لا») is the strongest text for this step; dropped by the refiner, Jami has a clean form (al-Sadiq, al-Kafi 1): restore as ASK (diag Q23). Import B7.L.5 (TC) supports it.
  2. What then is «إن الله شاء أن يراك قتيلا»? A command to the Imam, not a creative compulsion: one does not tell a leaf "stay green, God wills to see you green", but one tells a believer "use the miswak, God wills to see you fragrant"; over the killers' act God gave creative permission (He did not stand between Yazid and his will, in a world of test) with no pleasure in it; Shimr is not a wolf, he could have done what Hurr did; God never called Shimr to Karbala, He told him not to go and to turn back on the morning of Ashura. Two answers that read as one event, not a contradiction. (EZ H1 P186:15-29; P125:1-25; P146:19-25; P147:1-10; JS H1 P123:4-15; JS H1 P170:22-27; EZ H1 P113:1-11; EZ H1 P140:1-21 for "no joy in the Imam's death", TC; B7.I.63, JS-P18-S7). [B7.I.63 NEW (the "contradiction" mark lifted: diag Q3, ASK) | B7.I.64 NEW (Teacher Copy): «شاء أن يراك مصليا / حاجا / مخمسا» as everyday legislative wording (EZ H1 P180:14-22, P144:3-14) and «إن الله شاء أن يراهن سبايا» for the captives (EZ H1 P182:11-26); khums, Mutahhari and the dream rows fine layer] Imports B7.I.60, B7.I.24, B7.L.31, B7.L.1 (TC) support it.
  3. And «ما رأيت إلا جميلا»? What God did is beautiful; the killers' act is not His act, "you are not my God"; He is pleased that Husayn and Zaynab carry out their duty, not with the act of Shimr and Umar b. Sa'd. (EZ H1 P121:1-2, P122:1-21; JS H1 P121:5-23 one line in the TC; B7.I.62). [B7.I.62 NEW (ledger layer: Teacher Copy; ASK whether this one line goes to the Handout); the town destroyed by its people's choice (JS H1 P116, 17:16) to U7.27 or the fine layer (diag Q9)] Import B7.I.81 (TC) supports it.
  4. Does «يهدي من يشاء ويضل من يشاء» compel? No. Guidance has two senses: showing the way, which He gives to all through the prophets, and bringing to the goal, which He gives to those who keep taqwa; guidance is initial and consequential, misguidance only consequential and earned, «فأما من أعطى واتقى ... فسنيسره للعسرى». One scene only (the shopkeeper who directs his customer and misdirects the cheat, or the petrol pump). (EZ H1 P150:12-103 or EZ H2 P202:5-33, one telling; JS H1 P148:1-36 with 92:5-10 trimmed; JS H1 P149:1-10; B7.I.65, B7.I.87). [B7.I.65 NEW | B7.I.87 NEW] AH's three guidance records here are SAME and recalled (RR2-012, RR1-033, RR1-047, below); RR1-046 (NONE) in the TC.
  5. Is there a creative will over people that leaves them free? The purification verse: legislation is for all («يريد الله أن يطهركم», «يريد الله بكم اليسر»), but in 33:33, by «إنما», His creative will keeps filth from the Ahl al-Bayt, yet they stay free; the new part of 33:33 is idhhab as keeping away before it comes (the closed car window, not the scrub in the bath) and the continuous «يريد»; the father who washes his child himself against the one who tells a guest to wash shows creative against legislative purifying. (EZ H1 P157:3-15, P165:1-8, P168:1-4; JS H1 P160:1-13; EZ H2 P217:48-52, P217:62-63; EZ H2 P219:4-23; B7.I.67, B7.I.92, B7.I.93). [B7.I.67 NEW | B7.I.92 PART: recall B1 S14 "by 33:33 God removed rijs, doubt, from the Ahl al-Bayt (al-Sadiq: rijs is doubt)", then new: idhhab against izala and the continuous will, in EZ H2 P217:4-63, the inner tathir by the One who knows every impurity (P218) fine layer | B7.I.93 NEW] B7.I.94 (Ibn Hajar) and B7.I.95 (Tabatabai) Teacher Copy only.
  6. Is outward honour or power a sign of His will and approval? No. «تعز من تشاء» and «يهدي من يشاء» do not make Mu'awiya or Yazid honoured by God; honour given is a test: Zaynab in Yazid's court, «أظننت يا يزيد ...» with 3:178; and people's wish is not His wish: halal and haram are not a matter of majority, rule is from His appointment. (EZ H2 P203:11-18, P203:31-46; EZ H2 P223:32-33, P225:4-9, P226:10-12; B7.I.88, B7.I.96). [B7.I.88 NEW | B7.I.96 NEW (ledger layer: Teacher Copy; the one claim P225:6-7 may close the step as one line, ASK); the four routes and the Umayyad history (P222:4 to P223:37, P226:14-16) Teacher Copy only] AH's caliphate records: RR4-014 and RR4-016 PART (TC), RR5-018 SAME (recalled).
  7. Close: the occultation is the living case of "commanded, not loved": He commanded the Imam to be hidden as He commanded prayer, yet it is not loved, by Him or by the Imam, who asks «اللهم أنجز لي ما وعدتني»; it comes of people's acts, and the Imam bears it with the patience of Ayyub. One paragraph only. (EZ H2 P205:4-6, P208:5-15, P210:5-20, P212:6-13; B7.I.90). [NEW (doctrine wording ASK, diag Q17)] B7.I.91 (Fatima's outcry, 4:148) Teacher Copy only.

Next lesson picks up

اللہ کا کلام کیا ہے: اس کی ذات کی صفت، جیسے علم، یا اس کا فعل جسے وہ پیدا کرتا ہے؟ (What is God's speech: an attribute of His Essence like knowing, or an act He brings into being?) (S17)

6. His speech S17 to S20

S17 اللہ کا کلام اس کی ذات کی ازلی صفت نہیں بلکہ اس کا فعل ہے: وہ زبان اور ہونٹ کے بغیر جس مخلوق میں چاہے آواز اور حروف پیدا کرتا ہے، اور اسی طرح اس نے موسیٰ علیہ السلام سے، رسول اللہ صلی اللہ علیہ و آلہ سے اور اپنی ساری مخلوق سے کلام کیا

ledger H17 · 14 new · 3 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 1 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Is speaking an attribute of His essence, like knowing? No: al-Sadiq to Abu Basir, «إن الكلام صفة محدثة ليست بأزلية، كان الله عز وجل ولا متكلم»; knowledge is His essence, speech is an act; the Imam who denied that God is a body also denied that speaking is an essential attribute (JS's reading); an act attribute can be negated of Him, an essential one cannot. (MM H1 P001:5-6 = JS H1 P003:1-6 cut to the speech exchange; JS H1 P004:4-7, P008:8-19, P005:4 (Amali al-Tusi); JS H2 P107:27 (the Hadi letter: «كان عز وجل ولا متكلم ولا مريد»); JS H1 P014:5-6; JS H3 J.38 section (JS 7.66.2); B7.J.1, B7.J.38). [NEW; J.38 PART: recall B6 S01 "the Kafi report against the claim that God is a body (TC only)" then new: the same Imam also denied speech as an essential attribute]
  2. Then what is His speech? An act He brings into being; were it eternal it would be a second god (Nahj 186: «وإنما كلامه سبحانه فعل منه أنشأه ومثله لم يكن من قبل ذلك كائنا ولو كان قديما لكان إلها ثانيا»); reason says the same; and al-Kazim: «والكلام غير المتكلم». Teacher Copy: 31:27, His words are His creative words (EZ). (MM H1 P010:1-4 and JS H1 P013:1-2 as one block; JS H1 P012:6-12, P016:6-15; JS H2 P072:18 with P072:4-6 as setting, the claimant unnamed per Ask; EZ U7.38-2 P050:2-4 for B7.J.62; B7.J.2, B7.J.39, B7.J.62). [J.2 core repeat kept in full; J.39 PART: recall B6 S01 "al-Kazim: «أما علم أن الجسم محدود والكلام غير المتكلم», read there against God being a body (TC only)" then new: its use for speech being other than the Speaker, so originated]
  3. Was He then silent before He spoke? No: silence is only after speech; a lamp is not called silent (al-Rida to 'Imran); and the du'a «يا من فعله قول وقوله أمر». (MM H2 P120:1-2, P121:1; B7.J.7, B7.J.8). new
  4. How does He speak without tongue or lips? He creates sounds and letters, in any language and in any creature, without deliberation: «يخبر لا بلسان ولهوات ... يقول ولا يلفظ»; al-Kazim to the monk «أمر بلا شفة ولا لسان ولكن كما شاء أن يقول له كن فكان»; al-Rida to Abu Qurra «كلام الخالق لمخلوق ليس ككلام المخلوق لمخلوق ولا يلفظ بشق فم»; AH: «شيء لا كالأشياء». Close of the step: Amir al-Mu'minin, «فكلام الله تبارك وتعالى صفته وكلام البشر أفعالهم فلا تشبه كلام الله بكلام البشر», with JS's reading of «صفته» as "attributed to Him, not an essential attribute". (MM H1 P020:3, P021:1-3, P023:10; AH H1 P022:2-5, P062:1-11; JS H1 P027:6-14 (one line), P024:6-12; JS H1 P031:13 the block with P031:9 as setting in prose; JS H3 P125:13-16 one line; MM H2 P061:8 printed once with JS H2 P070:4-9; B7.J.4, B7.J.5, B7.J.20). new
  5. Where did Moses hear the call? From the tree, which did not speak: God created the voice in it, «لأن الله عز وجل أحدثه في الشجرة ثم جعله منبعثا منها»; 28:30 «من الشجرة». (EZ H1 P038:8-11, P039:9-19, P041:1-3; JS H1 P034:9 the key line; B7.J.6, Teacher Copy). [NEW; RR3-009 SAME: recall only]
  6. To whom has He spoken, and how? In three ways (42:51): revelation in the heart, from behind a veil, by a messenger, and all of it is His speech: «فإن معنى كلام الله ليس بنحو واحد»; one line: He does not need Gabriel to reveal. (JS H2 P089:9-24, P091:1-13, or EZ H2 P085:12-30, one of the two; 42:51 printed once for the Fasl; EZ H4 P156:22-23; B7.J.41, B7.J.57). new
  7. Did He speak to our Prophet, and to us all? At the mi'raj He addressed the Prophet in 'Ali's voice, «أنا شيء ليس كالأشياء ... فخاطبتك بلسانه كيما يطمئن قلبك»: a voice He created, in 'Ali's tone (lughat as lahja); "I never heard anything sweeter than my Lord's speech", "I spoke to you as I spoke to Moses". At the covenant He spoke to all His creation and they answered. He who made everything speak makes skins and limbs speak (41:21; the pebbles in Abu Jahl's hand, 36:65). The believer's true dream is like speech his Lord speaks to him. Teacher Copy: the heavens heard the revelation like iron on rock. (MM H1 P043:2-4 cut, AH H1 P044:3-4, EZ H1 P045:11-14; MM H1 P048:1-3 two clauses only; MM H1 P058:4-11, P058:2-3; MM H1 P060:11, EZ H1 P042:5-17; MM H3 P123:4; MM H2 P122:1; B7.J.9, B7.J.10, B7.J.14, B7.J.15, B7.J.13, B7.J.11). [J.9 PART: recall B5 S07 and B6 S06 "AH told (TC only) that at qab qawsayn the voice was 'Ali's: 'I speak, but in 'Ali's tone because you love 'Ali'" then new: the report as text and its reading for speech, a voice He created (AH P044:3-4, EZ P045:11-14); J.14 SAME: recall only; J.10, J.15, J.13, J.11 NEW; RR5-001 SAME: recall only]
  8. Close: His speech is His act, and the act shows its Doer: al-Sadiq, «لقد تجلى الله لخلقه في كلامه ولكنهم لا يبصرون», with AH's line that nothing issues from Him, so this is manifestation, not a part of Him coming down. One line only; the principle "every speaker's speech is his manifestation" is taught in S19 (B7.J.35, H19). (MM H1 P061:9-10 or AH H1 P002:4 in the Jami wording; AH H1 P062:4-9). [NEW; closing line]
Asks for Ali 24

Next lesson picks up

قرآن حادث ہے تو ائمہ علیہم السلام نے اسے «مخلوق» کہنے سے کیوں منع کیا؟ (The Quran is originated; why then did the Imams refuse to call it "created"?) (S18)

S18 قرآن اللہ کا کلام اور اس کا فعل ہے، اس لیے حادث ہے اور ازلی نہیں؛ ائمہ علیہم السلام نے اسے «مخلوق» اس لیے نہیں کہا کہ لغت میں مخلوق کا معنی گھڑا ہوا اور پرانا ہو جانے والا بھی ہے، اور قرآن نہ جھوٹا ہے نہ پرانا ہوتا ہے

ledger H18 · 8 new · 0 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 6
  1. Why did Muslims ever ask whether the Quran is creator or created? Because "God spoke" seemed to bring something new from the eternal, the question became "eternal or originated"; "creator" stood for eternal, "created" for originated; the debate grew among the other schools and the rulers of the time fed it, while the Imams kept to the Prophet's way (EZ P097:3-18, P095:10-21 no names, P100:2-4, P099:1-8, P093:9-11; JS P092:2-12, P096:1-15, P018:3-7; J.48, ledger layer teacher: the question in the Handout in two sentences, the history in the Teacher Copy). new
  2. What did the Imams answer? Al-Rida: "neither creator nor created, but the speech of God, Mighty and Majestic" (JS P019:1-6 with P018:5, the Bab's single home of this report); al-Kazim: "I do not say what they say; I say it is the speech of God" (JS P104:2-10, one block); Amir al-Mu'minin to the Khawarij: "by God, I did not make a creature arbiter, I made the Quran arbiter", the setting in one sentence (JS P145:4-12, P146:4-11; J.52). The answer is in the last clause: His speech is His act, so it is other than Him (JS P105:35-39, P104:10-11, P102:20-22, P018:8-9). new
  3. Does "not created" then mean eternal? No: the same letter says "the Quran is God's speech, originated, not created, and not eternal with God", and "He was, and not speaking, not willing, not acting; all these attributes are originated when the act is originated" (JS P107:7-16, P107:12, P107:27-29; J.50); the other letter: "there is no creator but God, and all besides Him is created, and the Quran is God's speech" (JS P131:4-13, P132:5-7; J.40); those who called it eternal held an inner speech (kalam nafsi) one with the Essence, and no one finds in himself any such thing besides knowledge, will and the letters he makes (JS P094:14-29, P142:2-12, P143:2, short, the Tawdih al-Murad Arabic in Urdu only; J.49). new
  4. Why is it originated? Because it is His act (one line recalling S17's Nahj 186 «وإنما كلامه فعل منه أنشأه», JS P115:3, P117:2), and because of what it holds: earlier and later parts, abrogating and abrogated (JS P149:2-8, P149:12-13); reports of Pharaoh's «أنا ربكم الأعلى» and Nuh's «يا بني اركب معنا» that would be false before they spoke (JS P150:2-20); verses God could take away or replace (17:86, 2:106: JS P150:21-23, P151:2-6, one telling; P117:1 the duplicate) (J.53, its Saduq part; the three Saduq subs have no point line). Saduq's own Arabic (P149:7, P150:18-20, P151:6) in Urdu only. new
  5. Then why avoid the word "makhluq"? Because in the language it also means forged (makdhub) and worn out, and the Quran is neither: "not created" means "not false", not "not originated" (JS P113:5-18 with 41:42 inline once, Saduq P109:8-9 and P115:4-5 in Urdu); and it does not wear out: «لا يخلق على طول الرد ولا ينقضي عبره ولا تفنى عجائبه» (JS P116:2-15, P106:2-9, P119:1-7, P117:12 as the last clause of the same letter, P118:7-21 one line); the same two reasons in the other sessions merged here (JS P017:6-10, P146:12-19, P147:2-3, P148:11-13; J.53, J.50). EZ adds a step: khalq needs matter, the Quran is from the world of command, an ibda' «لا من شيء», so originated yet not makhluq (EZ P110:7-11, P112:2-29; P111:6 printed in Urdu only, see Texts). The other two reasons (concession to the other side, P147:6-10; dalala as i'tibari, P148:3-10) in the Teacher Copy (diag Q9). new
  6. So what is the Quran? Al-Sadiq: "God's speech, God's word, God's book, God's revelation and His tanzil, the mighty book that falsehood does not approach from before it or behind it": true from end to end (JS P144:5-15; J.51); His act, and as every act shows its doer, His manifestation: one line recalling S17's close (AH P071:3-5 as the reconciling line, AH P002:4 "the originated attribute of the Kafi reports is this Quran"), printed as one reconciled account, not two rival views; «تنزل» (AH P011:6, P128:9) Teacher Copy only (J.3). Hand-off: if the Quran is His speech, are its very words His? [NEW; J.3 reconciliation per diag Q2]

Texts and Jami results 7
  1. «يا ابن رسول الله أخبرني عن القرآن أخالق أو مخلوق؟ فقال: ليس بخالق ولا مخلوق ولكنه كلام الله عز وجل» (al-Rida to al-Husayn b. Khalid; al-Tawhid, Amali al-Saduq 391, Bihar 89:117; JS P019:1-6, P018:5). Jami FOUND; the inline P103:3-4 follows Mustadrak Safina 8:459, which drops «ولا مخلوق»: print the Bihar wording once.
  2. «فقال قوم إنه مخلوق وقال قوم إنه غير مخلوق ... أما إني لا أقول في ذلك ما يقولون ولكني أقول إنه كلام الله» (al-Kazim to Sulayman al-Ja'fari; Amali al-Saduq 395-396; JS P104:5-9). Jami FOUND as printed.
  3. «القرآن كلام الله محدث غير مخلوق وغير أزلي مع الله تعالى ذكره ... كان عز وجل ولا متكلم ولا مريد ولا متحرك ولا فاعل ... فجميع هذه الصفات محدثة عند حدوث الفعل منه ... والقرآن كلام الله غير مخلوق فيه خبر من كان قبلكم وخبر ما يكون بعدكم» (JS P107:12, P107:27-28, P117:12 = P017:1). Jami FOUND-OTHER-SPEAKER: the letter is Imam al-Sadiq's, written for 'Abd al-Malik b. A'yan (al-Tawhid; Bihar 54:84, 89:119; Mustadrak Safina 4:247), not the letter of Imam al-Hadi as the ledger and diagnosis say (Ask 9). P017:1 and P117:12 are clauses of this same letter: one block.
  4. «وليس الخالق إلا الله عز وجل وما سواه مخلوق والقرآن كلام الله» (the letter of Imam al-Hadi, al-Tawhid bab al-Quran ma huwa no. 4; JS P131:4-6, :11). Jami FOUND.
  5. «والله ما حكمت مخلوقا وإنما حكمت القرآن» (Amir al-Mu'minin to the Khawarij; al-Tawhid no. 6, Bihar 33:381, Nahj; JS P145:4-7). Jami FOUND-OTHER-WORDING for the setting: «ألا إني أول من آمن بالله ورسوله», «فقالوا أنت كذلك» found; the printed «ولكنك حكمت في دين الله» not found as printed (Bihar has «حكمت في دين الله»).
  6. «وهو الذكر الحكيم والنور المبين والصراط المستقيم ... لا يخلق على طول الرد ولا ينقضي عبره ولا تفنى عجائبه» (al-Sadiq from his fathers; Tafsir al-'Ayyashi 1:74-81; JS P119:1-7). Jami FOUND. (B5 S01 prints a block of the same 'Ayyashi narration that stops at «ليس بالهزل», without the «لا يخلق» clause; not counted as a reprint, Ask 12.)
  7. «هو كلام الله وقول الله وكتاب الله ووحي الله وتنزيله وهو الكتاب العزيز الذي لا يأتيه الباطل من بين يديه ولا من خلفه» (al-Sadiq; al-Tawhid no. 3, Amali al-Saduq 390; JS P144:7-9, with 41:42). Jami FOUND; the transcript's «يا رسول الله» and «فصل الله» read as Jami «يا ابن رسول الله», «وحي الله». Jami results for the other texts: P094:7 «و يتكلم المجيب» is FOUND-OTHER-WORDING (the al-Hadi letter: «ويتكلف المجيب ما ليس عليه»); P131:2-3 «انما كلامه فعله» is not a narration (only Taqrib al-Ma'arif; the narration is Nahj 186, in S17); P111:6 «كلام الله لا خالق ولا مخلوق» (EZ's wording) is not in Jami (al-Baqir to Zurara «لا خالق ولا مخلوق ولكنه كلام الخالق», al-'Ayyashi, Bihar 89:120); P116:24-29 «لا جديد لمن لا خلق له» FOUND (Makatib al-Rasul 2:139); P144:2 is al-Rida to al-Rayyan in other wording («كلام الله لا تتجاوزوه ولا تطلبوا الهدى في غيره فتضلوا»); P144:19-20 FOUND (al-Tawhid no. 4) but carries no claim; P146:13, P148:11 «ليس بعزلي» is the garbled «وغير أزلي مع الله» of text 3; P145:11 «لما وزعت ان تزع الحرب اوزارها» NOT-FOUND.
Next lesson picks up

اگر قرآن اللہ کا کلام ہے تو کیا اس کے الفاظ بھی اللہ کے ہیں، یا صرف معانی؟ (S19)

S19 قرآن کے معانی ہی نہیں، الفاظ بھی خدا کے ہیں اور پیغمبر صلی اللہ علیہ و آلہ اس کے قاری ہیں؛ اس کا کلام اللہ ہونا اس کے اپنے علم سے بدیہی ہے، کیونکہ وہ علم الٰہی ہے جو بشر کے سیکھے ہوئے علم سے جدا ہے

ledger H19 · 15 new · 4 part-taught before · 2 taught before · 2 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. Whose are the Quran's letters and words, God's or the Prophet's? God's; the Prophet is the reciter: Jibril said «اقرأ», not "speak", and «اقرأ باسم ربك» followed (AH P002:1-15, P003:1-8, with 17:106 and 10:15 once); the repeated «قل» shows the words are given (MM P001:1, JS P004:2-9, EZ P005:11-21 one line); God the teacher, the Prophet the schoolchild who reads, Jibril bringing the slate (AH P044:3-9, J.29); the parrot (AH P045:1-4, J.30, Teacher Copy); the reciter may not add or drop a letter, «الم» was God's choice (RR5-011); 9:6 «حتى يسمع كلام الله» means these very words (AH P003:5, JS P007:4-10, P007:18-25, the block once). [J.16 core repeat: recall B4 S02 and B1 S10 "MM: from beginning to end the Quran speaks in the tongue of divinity, first addressing the one told to read and call" then kept in full; J.29 NEW; J.30 NEW; RR5-011 SAME: recall only]
  2. What do the words "Quran", "qira'a" and "kitab" themselves show? Qira'a is joining letters and words: «فإذا قرأناه فاتبع قرآنه» (75:18), «سنقرئك فلا تنسى» (87:6) (JS P133:1-12, P134:2-26; J.42); "Quran" and "kitab" name content and wording together (27:6, 2:101, 43:3; JS P135:2-25, P136:5-32; J.43); "had he forged sayings against Us" (69:44-46 in Tanzil wording, JS P137:3-17 with P004:18-22 one telling; J.44); the very words came down from the Tablet through the angels to his heart, 41:2, 39:1 (AH P052:1-4, P053:1-5; JS P055:3-6, P056:6-8, P057:1-10; MM P023:7-8 with 52:33-34 and 74:24-25; J.12); the one Quran sent down from One (JS P073:3-9; J.46, Teacher Copy); suras given in place of the Torah, verses clinging to the Throne (JS P138 to P141; J.45, Teacher Copy); the night plots answered by morning ayat (RR4-024) and "the tongue they point to is foreign, and this is clear Arabic" (16:103, RR4-025). [J.42, J.43, J.44 NEW; J.12 PART: recall B6 S02 "JS: the chain of revelation from Israfil through the angels, all of it God's speech" then new: the very words, not only the meanings, came down, in AH P052:1-4, P053:1-5 and JS P055:3 to P057:10; J.45, J.46 NEW (TC); RR4-024 SAME: recall only; RR4-025 PART: recall then new 16:103]
  3. Is there a witness from outside the Quran? The earlier book: "I will put My words in his mouth" (Deuteronomy 18; AH P046:1-4 with 19:97; J.31); but of the Torah only the ten commandments on the tablet were God's words, «فيها حكم الله» (5:43), and the present Torah and Gospel are not His speech (AH P047:1-13; J.32), «اختراع» rendered as Musa's own wording (Ask 9), with EZ P023:2-5 (the earlier books as hadith qudsi) reconciled in a teacher note. The Torah's God with a mouth breathing fire yet freezing water (RR3-019) under its recall. [J.31, J.32 NEW; RR3-019 SAME: recall only]
  4. How do we know it is God's speech? It proves itself: speech shows its meaning, and the Quran's meanings are sciences, wisdom and laws plainly of divine knowledge, which differs in kind from taught human learning; so "it is God's speech" is self-evident, and so the challenge works (MM P030:1-5, P008:1-6; AH P031:1-8; J.17 lines); it calls to God and what calls to God is from God (13:1, AH P009:1-8; RR2-015); «فاعلموا أنما أنزل بعلم الله» (11:13-14, JS P006:2-8); a verse is a descent from a boundless source, like rain from a cloud, not a completion of it, «تنزيل من رب العالمين» (EZ P010:18-26, P011:1-9); human speech uses words for inner meanings, His speech is His manifestation, so nothing resembles it (MM P061:3-7, P023:48-49; AH P062:10-11; the Burhan hadith P061:8 recalled in one line from S17); every speaker's speech is his manifestation and weighs as he does, so no translation carries it (AH P071:3-5 with P126:1 to P129:6 one line, P031:9-24 with 11:44; JS P065:11-20); one image only: «المرء مخبوء تحت لسانه» (AH P127:9, منسوب; J.37); the Walid report (MM P061:5-6); the Prophet unlettered among a people of poetry and star lore (RR2-032); news of the unseen fulfilled, 108:3 and Abu Lahab (RR4-030); bayan as His great gift (J.54, Teacher Copy under its recall); the Kindi story (J.47, Teacher Copy). [J.18 core repeat: recall B1 S12 "MM: the Quran must be judged by whether it is divine knowledge, which in itself differs from taught human knowledge" then kept in full, new AH 13:1 and EZ's descent; J.19 PART: recall B1 S10 "MM: as nothing resembles God, nothing resembles His speech, so it is inimitable" then new: human speech is words for inner meanings while His is His manifestation, in MM P061:7 and AH P062:10-11, JS P065:11-20; J.35 PART: recall B2 S10 "MM: in reciting, the servant finds God manifest in His speech" then new: AH's principle that every speaker's speech is his manifestation and weighs as he does, in AH P071:3-5, P128:6-9, and JS's reading P065:11-20; J.36 NEW (TC); J.37 NEW (TC, one image); J.47 NEW (TC); J.54 SAME: recall only; RR2-015, RR2-032 SAME: recall only; RR4-030 NONE, taught]
  5. What does divine knowledge look like in the Quran? All its sciences on one horizon, unlike human books ordered in chapters of uneven level (MM P039:1-3; J.23, under its recall); interwoven, "scattered of the scattered, gathered of the gathered" (RR4-026); spreading in two arcs (MM P040, J.24, teacher note); with an outer and an inner side, «له ظهر وبطن فظاهره حكم وباطنه علم» (MM P030:15, one short step; AH P034:1-5), the inner held by its bearers, the rest of the hermeneutics held for Ali (diag U7.38 Q2). The SAME records on the inner sense (RR2-005, RR4-023, RR5-012, RR3-017, RR3-018, RR4-031, RR5-024) sit under their recalls. [J.23 SAME: recall only; J.24 NEW (teacher note); RR2-005, RR4-023, RR4-026, RR5-012, RR3-017, RR3-018, RR4-031, RR5-024 SAME: recall only]
  6. Is all that God gave the Prophet of one rank? No: the Quran (words cast, recited, tanzil, the challenge), the hadith qudsi (God's words, not of the challenge), the sunna (meaning from God, words his own); all three are revelation and proof (AH P021:1-7 with 26:193-194 and 41:2; EZ P022:5-46 one example, P023:2-14, P026:8-13, 53:3-4); MM's king who speaks in royal address and privately to his vizier (MM P029:2-3, MM-P02-S4) recalled, not taught again. J.61 (rejecting any of these is kufr; "you are the city of knowledge" relayed from God) held, flagged here. [J.28 PART: recall B1 S10 "MM: the Quran and the hadith qudsi differ as a king's royal address and his private talk with his vizier" then new: AH's and EZ's three levels by words and meaning, in AH P021:1-7 and EZ P022:5-46, P026:8-13. Doubt read: B1 S10 (MM U1.16-4/P148:11) is the same MM passage as P029:3, so MM's side is SAME and the three levels are new; PART kept. J.61 NONE, held]
  7. Is the Quran's tongue itself unlike ours? The Imam calls Arabic "God's speech by which He speaks to His creatures" (MM P042:2-3, cut after «خلقه»; MM P041:3-6 on the origin of Arabic in the Teacher Copy, Ask 13); God, creator of eloquence, packs a book of meaning in one small word (AH P048:1-9, 55:19-20 only with Ali's word, Ask 14). Hand-off: if these are God's words, what happens when we recite them? [J.34 NEW]

Texts and Jami results 7
  1. «وَإِنْ أَحَدٌ مِّنَ الْمُشْرِكِينَ اسْتَجَارَكَ فَأَجِرْهُ حَتَّىٰ يَسْمَعَ كَلَامَ اللَّهِ» (9:6; AH P003:5, JS P007, AH P046:3; one block). Ayah.
  2. «اقْرَأْ بِاسْمِ رَبِّكَ الَّذِي خَلَقَ ۝ خَلَقَ الْإِنسَانَ مِنْ عَلَقٍ» (96:1-2; AH P002:8-9), flagged reprint (B1 S09, B2 S06). The Prophet's «ما أنا بقارئ» (P002:7) is in Jami only as the Bukhari report that Dala'il al-Sidq 4:137 lists among fabricated hadiths (Ask 8).
  3. «فَإِن لَّمْ يَسْتَجِيبُوا لَكُمْ فَاعْلَمُوا أَنَّمَا أُنزِلَ بِعِلْمِ اللَّهِ» (11:13-14; JS P006:4-8). Ayah.
  4. «فَإِذَا قَرَأْنَاهُ فَاتَّبِعْ قُرْآنَهُ» (75:18) with «سَنُقْرِئُكَ فَلَا تَنسَىٰ» (87:6) and «وَلَوْ تَقَوَّلَ عَلَيْنَا بَعْضَ الْأَقَاوِيلِ» (69:44-46, Tanzil wording, not the recorded «تقبل») (JS P133, P134, P137). Ayat.
  5. «ما هو شعر ولكنه كلام الله الذي ارتضاه لملائكته وأنبيائه ورسله» (the Prophet to al-Walid; MM U7.37-1 P061:5-6). Jami FOUND-OTHER-WORDING: Tafsir al-Qummi 2:393-394, Nur al-Thaqalayn 5:455; the print has «ولكن» and drops «ورسله»; al-Qummi's narrative, no Imam's chain.
  6. «وهو كتاب فيه تفصيل وبيان وتحصيل ... وله ظهر وبطن فظاهره حكم وباطنه علم» (the Prophet; al-Kafi, Mir'at 12:479; MM P030:15). Jami FOUND; flagged reprint (B5 S01).
  7. «تعلموا العربية فإنها كلام الله الذي يكلم به خلقه» (al-Sadiq; al-Khisal via Mustadrak Safina 3:24-25; MM P042:3), cut after «خلقه». Jami FOUND (the cut clauses «ونظفوا الماضغين وبلغوا بالخواتيم» also found; sense unsure). Jami results for the other texts: «أنا مدينة العلم وعلي بابها» (EZ P022:47) FOUND (Ghayat al-Maram 260, Ibn al-Maghazili), EZ's reading "God said: you are the city" is his own; «المرء مخبوء تحت لسانه» (AH P127:9) FOUND (Nahj, hikma), print as منسوب per the scholar; al-Baqir «القرآن واحد نزل من عند واحد» (JS P073:3-6) FOUND (al-I'tiqadat 86-87, al-Kafi 2:593); «أعطيت السور الطوال مكان التوراة» (JS P139) FOUND ('Uqud al-Marjan); the verses clinging to the Throne (JS P141) FOUND (al-Kafi 2:584), read «اهبطن» not «انهبطن»; the Kindi report (P076) is Bihar 50:311 from Abu al-Qasim al-Kufi's Kitab al-Tabdil, not an Imam's words; the angel texts P051:1, P054:1-5 FOUND; «انتفعوا ببيان الله» FOUND (Nahj 176); RR5-011's lead-in is FOUND-OTHER-WORDING (al-Baqir to Abu Labid: «إن في حروف القرآن المقطعة لعلما جما»); «لقد تجلى الله لخلقه في كلامه» (MM P061:9, recalled from S17) FOUND only in late sources; MM P043:2 «تجلى بها لخلقه» is MM's own wording (Nahj 147 «فتجلى لهم سبحانه في كتابه»); «شيء لا كالأشياء» (AH P062:2-4) FOUND-OTHER-WORDING (S17 carries it).

جب یہ الفاظ خدا کے ہیں تو جب ہم قرآن پڑھتے ہیں، کیا خدا ہم سے بات کرتا ہے؟ (S20)

S20 «قرآن پڑھنے والے سے خدا خود بات کرتا ہے، اور اس کی بات سچی ہے»

ledger · 4 new · 2 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 1 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Whose speech is it that we read? God's, words and knowledge alike (opening recap of S19, one line). (S19 steps 1 to 3). [NEW: recall S19 within the Bab, one line]
  2. What happens when we read it? The Speaker is present to every reader in every state, sees the hidden and the open, and speaks to that state; even the unbeliever finds his unspoken words told («ويقولون في أنفسهم», 58:8, second half only); so you can say: this morning God spoke to me. MM P012:3-4, P012:6, P012:8-10 («تجلى الله لخلقه في كلامه» after the check); AH P013:1-7, P014:4-9, P014:21-36; EZ P016:10-14 (B7.J.22). [PART, CORE REPEAT kept in full: recall B1 S18 "EZ: when you read the Quran God speaks to you, through the Prophet" and B1 S12 "AH: in prayer you speak to God, in reading the Quran God speaks to you"; then new: MM's ground in the Speaker's presential, encompassing knowledge of every state, the unbeliever told his hidden words (58:8), AH's walk through the states (sick, sinner, proud, angry, ignorant; ruler and beggar), EZ's "this morning God spoke to me", in MM P012:3-10, AH P013:1-7, P014:21-36, EZ P016:10-14]
  3. What does this ask of the reader? A mouth fit for His speech and attention even where one does not understand: His word has no smoke of human thought (the olive of 24:35 whose oil burns without smoke), so the throat that recites should be kept from lies and the bread that feeds it lawful; and even without understanding one listens with respect, since it is the Creator who speaks. EZ P052:2-6 (B7.J.63), told once with EZ P016:15-26; JS P015:7-11, P015:16-19 (one line). [NEW for B7.J.63 (the smokeless word, 24:35, the lawful bread condition); the base image is SAME material: recall B1 S18 "EZ: God spoke to Musa from a tree and speaks to this umma through the throat; keep the throat clean" (EZ U1.19-5/P092:12-46), so EZ P016:15-26 sits under that recall, flagged]
  4. Where is this conversation clearest? In prayer: the surahs are God speaking to you, the dhikrs are your speech to Him. AH P028:1-2, P028:9-16 (B7.J.33). ↺ B1 S12 · B1 S16 · B4 S15 · B6 S07recall only, "Recall B1 S12: in prayer you speak to God, in reading the Quran God speaks to you" (also B1 S16, B4 S15, B6 S07 for «المصلي مناج ربه»); only the advice to recite varied surahs, AH P028:3-8, is new, Teacher Copy with an Ask
  5. How far can this go? Al-Sadiq repeated «إياك نعبد وإياك نستعين» in prayer until he heard it from its Speaker. EZ P053:5-7, P054:9-10 (B7.J.64). new
  6. What does the reader gain? God says to His angels «see how My servant delights in reciting My speech» and promises «read in My gardens and rise in My ranks»; the rising has no end, because His speech is His disclosure and His disclosure has no bound. MM P019:4-6 (cut to the delight and the promise); AH P020:2-3, P020:4-26 (B7.J.27). ↺ B1 S16Already taught: B1 S16 "AH: the reciter is told «اقرأ وارقه», rising for as long as God is God";New here: MM's report of God's delight in His servant's reciting (Tafsir al-Imam), and AH's ground that His speech is His endless disclosure, in MM P019:4-6, AH P020:19-26
  7. And on the Day? The Quran comes in human form, passes every rank, and God calls it «يا حجتي في الأرض وكلامي الصادق الناطق». AH P036:2-15 (cut to the call), MM P035:1-5 (B7.J.26). new
  8. Since it is His truthful word, what is it to belie it? «قلنا صدق الله وقالوا كذب الله»: the deepest quarrel was over God's truthfulness, not tribe; the Kawthar example shows it (108:3, and the plan to cut the Prophet's line so as to say "God lied"); even those who allow Him every foul act do not ascribe a lie to Him. EZ P055:5-10 (one paragraph), EZ P055:12 to P062 (Teacher Copy), EZ P064:10 (one line, Teacher Copy) (B7.J.65, B7.J.66). [PART for B7.J.65: recall B3 S18 "EZ: al-Sadiq said we and the house of Abu Sufyan were enemies in God: we said God spoke truth, they said God lied" (held block in B3 S18's Teacher Copy); then new: the al-As b. Wa'il taunt, the Kawthar and 108:3, and the Umayyad plan to make the ayah false, in EZ P055:12-19, P058:2-4, P058:23-30, P059:8-18, P057:1-9. NEW for B7.J.66 (Teacher Copy)]

Next lesson picks up

«حکیم کے کام میں حکمت کہاں سے آتی ہے، اور کیا اس کا ہر کام بامقصد ہے؟» (Where does wisdom in the Wise One's acts come from, and is every act of His purposeful?) (S21)

7. His wisdom S21 to S22

S21 اللہ کا ہر فعل حکیمانہ ہے: وہ علم اور قدرت سے ہر چیز کو اس کی جگہ رکھتا ہے، اپنی کسی حاجت کے لیے پیدا نہیں کرتا، اور اس کی حکمت ہماری نظر سے چھپی ہو تب بھی برحق ہے

ledger · 17 new · 4 part-taught before · 1 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 8
  1. Where does wisdom in His acts come from? From knowledge with no ignorance joined to power with no defect: He knows the right and wrong of every thing, so He picks the wisest, and He can do all, so He does it; al-Rida's dua: He created creation by His power, perfected it by His wisdom and set each thing in its place by His knowledge, told once; and choosing is not acting without thought: a thousand considerations make up His far-reaching wisdom (JS U7.39-1 P017:1-11 with U7.39-2 P034:3-4 merged into one telling; EZ U7.39-1 P018:14-17; N.14, N.13). [NEW; AH's "the lower is for the higher, nothing is futile" (RR5-002, RR5-003), "each thing by measure" (RR3-028) and the ant's granary (RR4-040): SAME, recall only]
  2. If He never acts against wisdom, is He unable to? He can and does not: wisdom bounds the act, not the power; He can wrong and does not («إن الله لا يظلم الناس شيئا», 10:44, one line), He does not break His promise, and His will follows His wisdom with no compulsion in it; no means turns back His wisdom (EZ U7.39-1 P022:7-9, 11-12, 15-17, 19, 24-28, with a neutral example, "a man of sense can do many senseless things and never does", in place of the hall image; JS P020:4-6, P021:2-14, P020:14-20; U7.3-2 JS P084:2-3, Sahifa 13 in Jami's wording; N.16, B.16). new
  3. Is He then bound to one "best" order? No: there is no proof that this order is the only best one; many orders may hold equal good, and among them His own will chooses; a free agent naturally takes the better, yet His hands are not tied; He is a choosing agent, not a necessitating complete cause (JS U7.39-1 P023:1-16, P020:3-12, P021:15-17, reconciled with U7.39-2 P028:3-10; JS U7.39-2 P026:10-13, 18-19 read as «علتِ تامہ»; MM U7.39-2 P027:9, creation and legislation alike by His ra'y, one line; N.17, N.28). [NEW; one Handout sentence, the rest Teacher Copy (diag Q5)]
  4. For whose sake does He act? Not for a need or gain of His own: if He needed His act He would be perfected by another, which every rational and textual proof rejects (JS U7.39-2 P025:7-13, P026:7-9, 12-13; N.26). [NEW; AH's "servanthood is our perfection, for God it makes no difference" (RR3-032): SAME, recall only]
  5. Then why did He create? Creation is mercy on the rational beings, and the rest of the world serves them on the path of servanthood (11:119, «ولذلك خلقهم»); good flows from the Good, as the rich and generous man gives with no need and no compulsion; the motive is not gain sought or harm averted but giving good to another, as one gives blood to a sick stranger; the benefits follow His act and are not its cause, so "why" is not put to Him; 51:56 (JS U7.39-2 P026:1-6, 18-28; EZ U7.24-2 P106:1-17, P107:1-15, P109:18-40; JS U7.39-3 P050:3-15, P049:3-8, one line each; K.36, N.27). [K.36 PART: recall B1 S01 "our knowing adds nothing to Him; He created so that His grace reaches the contingent", then new: EZ's motive against destination and the blood-giving example; N.27 NONE, Handout keeps only P050:6-15]
  6. Is creation then purposeful, not in vain? Yes: asked whether there was wisdom in creation or it was in vain, the Syrian himself answers "wisdom"; «إنك على صراط مستقيم»: all His acts stand on wisdom; and, in one line, wisdom requires a life beyond this one, else the creation of so capable a being would be futile; the promise and wisdom both demand the raising (JS U7.39-2 P035:12-14, P036:1-2; JS U7.39-3 P044:1-5; JS U7.22-2 P094:7-17 one line, the Tajrid sentence in Urdu only; N.15, N.18, N.24, H.69). [N.15 PART: recall B3 S19 "the Hisham and Syrian debate, used there as an opening", then new: the debate read for creation having a wisdom; H.69 PART: recall B3 S13 "the Just and Wise cannot leave oppressor and oppressed unreckoned", then new: the proof from His promise; N.18, N.24 NEW; AH on 23:115 (RR3-033) and the half-shop's accounts (RR1-039): SAME, recall only]
  7. Why do some of His acts look wrong to us? His wisdom is far-reaching and every part of the order is linked to the rest; we see one road, the planning office sees the whole country; much of His wisdom is hidden from us; Imam Hasan answers the critics of the peace: Khidr's acts angered Musa «إذ خفي عليه وجه الحكمة في ذلك», yet with God it was «حكمة وصوابا» (EZ U7.39-3 P052:1-22, 28-30, 35-39, 42, 54-60, told once; EZ P053:2-5, softened per diag Q7; EZ P054:3-9; N.51, N.52, N.53). [NEW; AH's "the peace was born of God's hidden wisdoms" (RR4-013): PART, Teacher Copy; AH's poison and honey from one jug (RR3-041) and the Indian ascetic (RR2-031): SAME, recall only]
  8. What does it ask of us? Patience without «لِمَ و بِمَ», and firm work: the Wise loves firm work, so the Prophet sealed the seams of the grave with clay (EZ U7.39-3 P052:43-53, the du'a line «وصبرني على ذلك» named; EZ P055:3-9, P056:19-20; N.54). new

  • Kept: B7.N.34 (MM 7.32.3, MM* MMB_07_S12 ¶527; JS; EZ 7.21.6), under step 2. Claude's judgement MM argues that if He is able to wrong and His ra'y falls on justice He is glorified as just; the atlas itself pairs this passage with "able against wisdom, He chooses wisdom" (N.16), so it supports step 2's claim directly: refraining is by choice, not by inability, and it is not compulsion.
  • Kept: B7.N.40 (MM* MMB_08_S01 ¶22-28, Ali on qadar "a deep sea, do not enter it"; EZ 7.22.5; JS), under step 7. Claude's judgement where the reason for an act of His, such as a child's death (EZ P052:61-63), is unseen, His justice and wisdom stand on what reason proved of Him and one does not delve; this supports step 7's "we see one road". The MM text is about qadar, a near but not identical subject, so the fit is fair, not strong (Ask 33).

اگر اللہ کا ہر فعل حکیمانہ ہے تو کیا اس کے احکام بھی حکمت پر مبنی ہیں، اور جب وہ جانتا ہے کہ کون نافرمانی کرے گا تو حکم کیوں دیتا ہے؟ (S22)

S22 «حکیم کا حکم بھی حکمت ہے: وہ جانچتا ہے، سزا دیتا ہے یا لطف کرتا ہے، اور ہم ہر حال میں مانتے ہیں»

ledger · 9 new · 5 part-taught before · 0 taught before · 0 kept in full
The steps 7
  1. Are His commands wise too, as His acts are (S21)? Yes: command and prohibition are His alone, and His command is decree and wisdom, decreed with knowledge; in «وقضى ربك ألا تعبدوا إلا إياه وبالوالدين إحسانا» the qada is His command, else even a son's abuse of his father would count as ihsan. JS H1 P003:1-10 (17:23 in Tanzil form); JS H3 P045:8, P046:3-6 («أمره قضاء وحكمة», «يقضي بعلم»); EZ U7.30-2 P199:2-12 (absolute legislative wilaya) (B7.N.25, B7.I.78). new
  2. Do benefits cause His commands? No: benefits and harms, even granted, are wisdoms the Commander observes, not causes that bind Him; He commands by His ra'y, and a benefit in the commanding itself is enough. MM H1 P001:4-5, P002:6; MM H2 P027:5 (one line); AH H1 P015:29-34 placed as what He observes, reconciled, not opposed (B7.N.1). new
  3. Then why command if He knows who will disobey? Every command is a test, not for Him to learn but for the servant to know himself in obeying and disobeying; and the command is His mastership performed, so the disobedient are truly addressed. MM H2 P027:4-5 (one telling; the H1 P005:3-6 duplicate dropped); MM H1 P006:6-7 (B7.N.3, B7.N.4 Teacher Copy). [PART for B7.N.3: recall B1 S06 "AH: God does not test to learn what He does not know; His test is so that reward and punishment rest on proof, «فلله الحجة البالغة»"; then new: MM's reason that every command is a test so that the servant knows himself, and the command as mastership, in MM P027:4-5, P006:6-7. NEW for B7.N.4 (Teacher Copy)]
  4. Is every ruling's wisdom the same? No: a test, a just recompense («فبظلم من الذين هادوا حرمنا عليهم طيبات أحلت لهم», 4:160), or plain grace («عفي عن أمتي ثلاث ... وهنا رابعة وهي ما لا يطيقون»); the bad that befalls me for my own deed is His just act, not His wrong. MM H1 P002:2-6, P001:4; EZ H1 P007:11-27 one line, reconciled (B7.N.5); AH re-read RR4-015, RR4-017, RR5-005, RR5-007 (carrying out a divine hadd brings down mercy) one line, with an Ask. new
  5. What if we do not know the wisdom? We obey: God is not asked how He made lawful; reason cannot reach why sa'y, harwala or the stoning, yet He commands nothing idle; and of two sons who come at their father's call, the one who came not knowing why has the higher worth, though knowing reasons can raise devotion. MM H1 P008:1-2, P008:16-17, P008:13 (last sentence only); AH H1 P009:2-24; EZ H1 P010:1-9, P011:3-14 (B7.N.6). new
  6. How do we come to know His commands? We cannot weigh what our acts do to body and spirit here and hereafter, so His wisdom and grace send prophets; even the bare probability that the Benefactor has pleasure, wrath and commands moves us to seek them; the God who did not neglect the hollow of the foot does not leave man without guidance, nor build a palace in a desert for no one. AH H1 P015:2-19, P015:29-37 (shortened); AH H2 P031:1-7; JS H1 P016:1-3 (one line); JS U7.41-1 P057:1-22 (one line); one pointer line to the Proof on earth, AH H1 P013:1-6 (Teacher Copy) (B7.N.9, B7.N.10, B7.O.32, B7.N.8, B7.N.7 Teacher Copy). [NEW for B7.N.9. PART for B7.N.10: recall B1 S16 "AH: reason must move to learn what the Owner and Benefactor wants" and B1 S01 "JS: avoiding probable harm is a rule of reason"; then new: AH's step that the mere probability of His pleasure, wrath and commands already obliges us to learn our duties, in AH P031:1-7. PART for B7.O.32: recall B1 S16 and B1 S11 "since we do not know what pleases Him or benefits us, He sends prophets"; then new: JS's framing, His wisdom gives man the means of growth, reason and prophets, the palace in a desert, in JS U7.41-1 P057:1-22. PART for B7.N.8 (Teacher Copy pointer only). NEW for B7.N.7 (Teacher Copy)]
  7. And Satan: why let the misleader loose? In his creation the servanthood of every rank, from the first emanated to the weakest creature, and God's greatness and mastership appear, so creation confesses «ما قدروا الله حق قدره»; and the God who left him free «إلى يوم الوقت المعلوم» set by His justice a fortress against him. AH H2 P033:1-4 (22:74); AH H2 P032:1-9 (38:80-81); the fortress named in one Handout sentence without the two unverified texts, P032:10-13 (Ask) (B7.N.12, B7.N.11). [PART for B7.N.12: recall B2 S16 "EZ: Satan was given reason and duty and yet left, and in God's refuge he cannot harm"; then new: AH's point that in Satan's creation the servanthood of every rank and God's mastership become manifest, in AH P033:1-4. NEW for B7.N.11]
